The Outer Worlds 2 PS5 DLC Split into Two Parts, First in December

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Obsidian still wants to carry out its post-launch DLC plans for The Outer Worlds 2, having lost 25% of its workforce in a recent round of layoffs. It's today announced an expansion called Side Effect Guaranteed will release in two parts: the first launches for PS5 on 2nd December 2026, and then the second arrives in Spring 2027. See it in action in the trailer.

The new DLC takes place just before the end of the main game, so if you've already finished a playthrough, there'll be a new option on the starting menu screen that lets you access the content through a new character.

A distress signal beckons you and your crew of the Incognito to a derelict ship, where you discover the ship’s crew in suspended animation. All signs point to foul play, especially the puddle of what was most likely a person that you recover a video message from. It was left by the captain of the vessel, who explains that the crew has been contaminated with something while on a heist that will cause explosive cell death unless they find a cure. And by explosive cell death, she means more like a… “liquefaction”.

Side Effect Guaranteed adds a new region to explore called Elysium Valley, another recruitable character, a level cap increase to 36, more than 15 new perks, four extra flaws, and additional gear (weapons, armour, and outfits). You can also look forward to an additional 20 songs.

The expansion costs $14.99, or if you bought the Premium Edition of the game, you'll gain instant access to the DLC.

Review The Outer Worlds 2 (PS5) - Mechanically Fantastic RPG Struggles to Be Memorable

It'll work to expand and improve an RPG we thought was pretty good at release, awarding it a 7/10 rating. "The Outer Worlds 2 succeeds in being a bigger and better sequel, buoyed by an even greater emphasis on player choice and freedom. Its RPG mechanics are largely fantastic, and there are key improvements to both combat and exploration," we said at the time. "However, despite Obsidian's clever writing, there's an underlying dreariness to the property that it just can't seem to escape."

Opinion: Sign him, Liverpool: South American star is screaming 'come and get me'

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

One South American Premier League star is just begging for Liverpool to sign him. It's time to get a deal done.

Liverpool have a selection of superstars in their squad right now. That's the result of an unprecedented spend over the last couple of years, with the Reds repeatedly breaking their transfer record.

It was viewed as the way to go post-Jurgen Klopp. They wanted to sign as much talent as possible, taking advantage of the position that Klopp left them in, essentially.

The result? It's not a great squad. The talent pool is fantastic, no question there, but Liverpool have been unable to piece things together to create a bigger picture.

It's why we're not sure that Liverpool's next big moves will be for players who glue things together, rather than lead the side. And that simply must point them strongly in one direction.

It's Diego Gomez againWhat do Liverpool need? Well, they need more energy on and off the ball. They need a right-winger. They need a midfielder. They need more space in attack. They could really use more defensive work. Congratulations! You (by which I mean I) have described Diego Gomez. The Brighton star is essentially everything Liverpool need right now. He's a right-sided midfielder who can also play through the middle. Not just as an attacking midfielder, either, but as a covering defensive midfielder, or a box-to-box. The Paraguayan also isn't a superstar who requires lots of the ball. Gomez is happy to take a backseat to others and play a supporting role - and an incredibly strong one.

What do Liverpool need? Well, they need more energy on and off the ball. They need a right-winger. They need a midfielder. They need more space in attack. They could really use more defensive work.

Congratulations! You (by which I mean I) have described Diego Gomez. The Brighton star is essentially everything Liverpool need right now.

He's a right-sided midfielder who can also play through the middle. Not just as an attacking midfielder, either, but as a covering defensive midfielder, or a box-to-box.

The Paraguayan also isn't a superstar who requires lots of the ball. Gomez is happy to take a backseat to others and play a supporting role - and an incredibly strong one.

There is a reason, after all, why Brighton rest Gomez in the UEFA Conference League. He's played all but 23 minutes in the Premier League for the side currently in second and is the kind of player that elite teams thrive with - someone who wins the ball back, makes lots of runs, and pops up with incredible moments.

We've seen two of those this season. The first was a ridiculous goal from halfway. The second was a sensational goal against Brentford this weekend that was essentially a "come and get me plea" to any top clubs looking for that 'glue' player.

We wrote back in August that Gomez is the kind of player Liverpool need. Our Value Adder model is one that pushes him as as an incredibly valuable footballer who's still only 23 and flying under the radar.

Now, he's no longer flying particularly far under that radar. People are picking him up thanks to these wonder goals. But with Brighton almost certainly facing massive offers for their bigger 'stars' in 2027, we think there's a chance to tempt them with an offer here.

Gomez is what Liverpool need. He's worth a fortune to a club like the Reds who are just a player or two away from having a very balanced team.

Serie A: AS Roma crushes Sassuolo and returns to one point of 1st place

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

AS Roma did not tremble! Opposed to Sassuolo this Saturday during the 11th day of Serie A, Gian Piero Gasperini’s men largely won at home (3-0) and achieved an excellent performance in the standings. Third before the match, the Romans had the opportunity to come back one step behind their historic rival, Lazio, leader of the championship. And they didn’t miss their chance. Dominant from the first minutes, the Giallorossi found the opening in the 27th minute thanks to Mario Hermoso. After a one-two with Donyell Malen and a mix-up in the opposing defense, the Spanish defender recovered the ball at the edge of the area before unleashing a powerful strike in the middle, deceiving Arijanet Muric (1-0). Three minutes later, Roma broke with some success. On a cross-shot from Malen, Sebastian Walukiewicz accidentally deflected the ball into his own net (30th, CSC, 2-0). A comfortable advantage that Gasperini’s players maintained until the break, controlling the debates and monopolizing the ball.

Returning from the locker room, the Romans continued their domination and did not take long to definitely make the difference. In the 57th minute, Rodrigo Mora completed a superb collective action initiated on the left side. After combining with Malen in a perfectly executed one-two, the young Portuguese attacking midfielder adjusted Muric with a cross shot to score his team’s third goal (3-0). A clear victory for Roma who completely stifled their opponent, with 27 shots attempted throughout the match and only one shot on target conceded. Established for the second consecutive time due to Mile Svilar’s injury, Colombian goalkeeper Devis Vásquez was hardly ever called upon. Thanks to this success, AS Roma temporarily climbs to second place in Serie A and ensures that it will spend the international break on the podium. For its part, Sassuolo falls to 17th place, just above the red zone, and could fall among the relegated if Monza wins against Atalanta. After the break, the Romans will travel to Parma, while Sassuolo will host Genoa.

It’s Grammy Night on Dancing With the Stars, and there’s a twist! On top of performing to award-winning songs, the remaining couples (minus Harry Shum Jr. and Jenna Johnson) are competing in “dance-offs,” where they’ll be performing the same dance style to the same song—at the same time. A little confused as to how the whole thing goes down? Here’s what to expect tonight.

As the show puts it in their official press release: “Two at a time, couples face off against one another on the ballroom floor as they dance head-to-head in the same style at the same time. Winners of each of the three Dance-Offs will be selected by the judges, and the winning couple from each dance-off will receive bonus points added to their judges’ totals for the night.”

Here’s an example from a few seasons back:

Here are the pairings, the songs, and the dance styles:

Amber and Pasha vs. Ezra and DaniBoth couples will perform a rumba to “The Boy Is Mine,” performed live by Brandy and Monica.

Jenna and Val vs. Tatyana and JanBoth couples will perform a cha-cha to “The Middle,” performed live by Maren Morris.

Connor and Rylee vs. Maura and MarkBoth couples will perform a cha-cha to “All I Wanna Do,” performed live by Sheryl Crow.

As far as we know (unless things have changed!), it comes down to the judges! The winner of each dance-off will get extra points added to their score—which could change the leaderboard before the public votes are factored into things.

These two have immunity thanks to getting the highest scores last week! Which means judges will automatically add the dance-off bonus points to their score this week.

Jimmy Carter’s Family Farm Just Sold for $8.5 Million

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Georgia property will become part of a nearly 7,000-acre wildlife management area, with public access possible as early as 2027.

President Jimmy Carter’s family farmland near Plains, Georgia, was just sold to the Conservation Fund as part of an approximately $8.5 million acquisition that will protect nearly 2,500 acres of fields and woodland. Announced November 9, the deal sets the stage for the land’s eventual transfer to the state of Georgia, with public access potentially beginning as early as 2027.

The purchase encompasses approximately 1,600 acres owned by the Carter family, along with an adjoining tract. It does not include Carter’s boyhood home and farm, which are already preserved by the National Park Service as part of Jimmy Carter National Historical Park.

Instead, the newly acquired property is slated to join the nearby Lanahassee Creek Wildlife Management Area, expanding it to nearly 7,000 acres. The land provides habitat for gopher tortoises and other wildlife, while neighboring creeks support rare freshwater mussels. Plans call for the Georgia Department of Natural Resources to take ownership in 2028.

The acquisition also continues restoration work Carter initiated himself. At his request, the family preserved a stand of mature longleaf pine and replanted much of the surrounding acreage with the same native species. Once used to grow crops, including peanuts, the property became a retreat where he and Rosalynn spent time away from public life. The family acquired the land around the turn of the 20th century, according to the Conservation Fund.

“They liked to go and walk the land,” Blake Sullivan, who managed Carter’s farm and timberland for more than four decades, told The Washington Post. “For both of them, it was a place of rest and renewal.”

Carter continued visiting into his final years, arranging to be driven through the property when walking became difficult. Amy Carter, the president’s daughter, told The Post, “I think his very favorite place was to be in the town and on the land where he grew up, and where he had those very deep roots.”

Find a gift that kids will actually like

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

When it comes to gifting, kids can be particular. The things kids like change not just with age, but also with evolving trends from TV shows and new technology. If parents are lucky enough, their kid will write a long list of the precise items they'd like as a birthday or Christmas or Hannukkah gift and put it up on the fridge with pride. But sometimes, it can be as much of a crapshoot as coming up with a gift idea for someone else's kid. Without further ado, here are the best gifts for kids of all ages — including toy and non-toy gifts alike.

Everyone is familiar with the "terrible twos" rep that the not-a-baby-but-not-yet-an-independent-kid stage of development gets. At this age, kids are bouncing constantly between the desire to explore on their own and the reliance on parents for most basic needs like eating, getting dressed, or going to the bathroom. That's a lot of frustrating emotions to go through in a day. Kitchen play sets, toys that involve different motor skills, and anything with music are gifts that can let toddlers feel in charge without too much assistance from a parent — making them ideal for this age category.

Potentially a more daunting phase than the terrible twos: The one where the pressure to be cool is relentless. This is that age. Though "cool" can lend itself to multiple interpretations, gifts that contribute to an older kid's evolving critical thinking skills and sense of independence, responsibility, and style are all smart ideas. These could be anything from STEM toys to beginner's makeup tools to a fitness tracker or a pretend pet that encourages routine-making. Anything that involves an app, tablet, or e-reader is also a guaranteed win.

Finding a gift that a teenager will think is cool (and that will be on-trend for longer than a few months) might be some of the trickiest shopping you'll do all year. They'll probably offer up a few big softball suggestions, but if you're going the surprise route, remember that they care about brands. Thanks to social media influencers promoting products directly to teens on Instagram or TikTok, Gen Z kids are loyal to very specific brands of tech, accessories, and even water bottles. If you follow the trends as closely as they do (trust us, we do), cracking the "best gifts for teenagers" code isn't rocket science.

Norway’s Electric Snowplow Worked. Sweden Added Hydrogen Anyway.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Norway has already demonstrated battery-electric snowplowing on one of its toughest mountain highways. A Scania 45 R covered roughly 7,000 km on Hardangervidda, plowing, grading and spreading sand through a Norwegian winter. Its performance exceeded expectations. Meanwhile, Sweden has unveiled a hydrogen snowplow that is, in fact, another large battery-electric truck, with a fuel cell and hydrogen tanks added for extra range. The engineering question is why that second energy system is worth having when batteries are already doing the work, and when a conventional generator running on renewable diesel could offer a simpler, potentially cheaper route to occasional extended operation.

The Swedish vehicle has to compete with more than diesel snowplows. My full TFIE Strategy Briefing examines Norway’s measured electric operation, China’s expanding snow-removal equipment market and the additional costs hydrogen must justify.

Sweden’s project brings together Scania, MaserFrakt and Peab. The vehicle carries a 356 kWh rechargeable battery and 56 kg of hydrogen. The fuel cell generates electricity to extend the truck’s operating time between charging sessions. That gives the vehicle two energy-storage systems, plus the equipment to convert hydrogen into electricity. It will still need electric charging, and it will also need hydrogen production, delivery, storage and refueling.

Norway’s experience makes the starting point uncomfortable for hydrogen advocates. The Norwegian Public Roads Administration’s 2025 trial demonstrated that a production-based heavy electric truck could do demanding winter-maintenance work. Its measured consumption averaged approximately 2.02 kWh/km, with around six and a half hours of operating endurance under evaluated mixed-duty conditions. The trial identified shortcomings in charging compatibility and payload, but these were engineering and operating problems to resolve, not evidence that battery-electric snowplows were incapable of doing the job.

China is moving toward broader deployment. Municipalities have used smaller electric snow-removal equipment for years, while manufacturers have developed full-sized specialized electric vehicles. Beijing’s Mentougou district awarded contracts for 24 multifunction snow-removal trucks in September 2026. The final chassis specifications have not all been confirmed as battery-electric, but the procurement demonstrates a market moving beyond isolated demonstrations.

China’s much larger battery-electric truck industry is also providing specialized equipment manufacturers with an expanding supply of powertrains, batteries and heavy-duty chassis. Electric snowplows benefit from components developed and manufactured for freight vehicles at much greater volumes.

The Swedish approach looks increasingly difficult to justify against that background. If six or seven hours of productive operation is adequate, a battery-electric truck can already do the work. If longer shifts are required, larger batteries, better charging arrangements, mobile charging equipment or operational changes deserve assessment first. Hydrogen cannot claim the extreme-duty market merely because charging requires planning.

There is another alternative that the hydrogen comparison tends to omit. A conventional generator running on hydrotreated vegetable oil, or HVO, could supply additional electricity when the battery needs support. Diesel generators, fuel tanks and liquid-fuel distribution systems are mature technologies. Major generator manufacturers already approve suitable equipment for HVO, which can use conventional diesel-type storage and handling systems. Integrating a generator into a particular electric truck still requires engineering, certification and emissions controls, but it avoids constructing a hydrogen supply chain for a small fleet of seasonal vehicles.

For some operators, the even easier solution is to retain existing diesel snow-removal equipment and use certified renewable diesel for the relatively few extreme shifts that electric vehicles cannot cover. Copenhagen Airport has already adopted that approach, progressively electrifying its equipment while using HVO in compatible heavy vehicles, including snow-removal machines.

HVO is not unlimited, and its climate benefits depend on feedstock origin and production. Burning it also produces exhaust emissions. These constraints are important, particularly in populated areas. But a relatively small quantity used for exceptional winter duty is a different proposition from trying to replace a large share of road transportation with biofuels.

The hydrogen proposal must beat all these alternatives. It needs to demonstrate that the additional hours gained from the fuel cell are worth the vehicle cost, maintenance, fuel premium and refueling infrastructure. It also needs to outperform the cost of providing those hours with conventional generating equipment or retaining a small reserve fleet.

Snow-removal authorities have an obligation to maintain roads during severe weather. They should purchase equipment that meets that obligation with the lowest defensible whole-system cost and emissions. Norway has shown that batteries can perform demanding winter work, and China’s industrial scale is making specialized electric vehicles more accessible. Sweden’s hydrogen truck is entering a market in which the simpler electric solution is already working.

The full TFIE Strategy Briefing analysis examines the operating results, procurement evidence, charging limitations, airport fuel-cell trials and the practical alternatives that make hydrogen’s business case so difficult.

GTA 6 will technically be playable on Xbox One at launch

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

On Tuesday, Rockstar Games gave us some details about how Grand Theft Auto VI will look and run on current-gen consoles. But there was a fascinating buried lede in that blog post.

Using Xbox Cloud Gaming, you'll be able to stream GTA 6 to an Xbox Series console or an Xbox One. I'm not entirely sure why you'd stream it to one of the newer consoles instead of just running it natively, but the option is there, nonetheless. If you play GTA 6 this way, the stream will use the Xbox Series S version's graphics settings.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Of course, there is one final very relevant detail here: Starting this month, Xbox has imposed strict hourly limits on cloud gaming. You can only stream games for five to 15 hours per month, depending on which version of Game Pass you pay for. There's also the matter of game streaming being subject to the whims of internet quality, so things like buffering, latency, and poor visual quality can be part of the experience.

So, it might still be worth it to just try and find a current-gen console instead.

Scientists figured out how to use a cheap everyday camera to measure how well solar panels work

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

New digital tools turn massive amounts of complex data into simple, actionable insights to help leaders make better conservation decisions.

Solar panels glow if you run electricity into them in the dark. While photos easily show the bright and dim patches, measuring the exact brightness used to require an expensive, manually calibrated industrial camera.

Jürgen Werner and her research team from the University of Stuttgart, the Research Center Jülich, and the German company Solarzentrum Stuttgart got the same kind of number out of a $450 consumer camera, after writing down how its own electronics respond to light.

The camera is the easy part. A model of how it converts light into numbers is what makes the reading possible, and that model still leans on one reference cell with a known quality.

Push current into a solar cell and it behaves like an LED, giving off light of its own. Silicon cells glow in the infrared range, which the eye can’t see, so the picture has to come from a camera that can.

Engineers already use those pictures to check solar panels for cracked cells and broken connections. What they read is contrast. Bright areas are doing their job, dark ones aren’t.

The brightness itself is a measurement. The fraction of electrons that leave a solar cell as light instead of heat is its luminescent quantum efficiency.

The cell’s open-circuit voltage – the voltage it holds when nothing draws power from it – determines that fraction. Every tenfold rise adds about 60 mV to that voltage.

“An electroluminescence image contains much more quantitative information than simply showing bright and dark regions,” said Werner, of the Institute for Photovoltaics at the University of Stuttgart.

“With a suitable physical camera model and calibration, it can provide absolute luminescent quantum efficiency and, therefore, information about the local quality of a solar cell or module.”

Werner worked with Georgette Udo at Forschungszentrum Jülich and Liviu Stoicescu at Solarzentrum Stuttgart, a German company that inspects photovoltaic systems. Their camera was a Canon EOS 4000D, a low-cost model selling for around €400 in 2026.

Most consumer cameras carry a film over the sensor that absorbs infrared light, so the team took that film out. Astronomy hobbyists make the same change to shoot the night sky.

Then they screwed a long-pass filter onto the lens, which blocks the visible light that would otherwise wash out the faint glow.

The researchers took every photograph in the dark. A current source pushed a known current through each module, and the shutter time, the aperture and the ISO setting decided how much glow reached the sensor.

Brightness was then averaged across a whole cell in ImageJ, free image software, by drawing a box over the cell by hand.

Here is the problem that the model had to solve. A camera doesn’t report light honestly all the way up.

Below a certain brightness the number a pixel returns rises in step with the light that hits it, but above it the pixel fills up and the response stops rising.

That is over-exposure, familiar from any photograph taken into the sun.

Werner’s team measured where their own camera crossed over. It stayed linear below a brightness value of 400, and above that it bent toward a ceiling, which for one module sat at 1,177.

Both stretches are usable once the shape of the curve is known, and the saturated one yields a second estimate of the same quantity.

Converting brightness into quantum efficiency needs an anchor. The team took theirs from a cell with an established efficiency – 1.6 percent at 738 mV – and scaled everything else against it.

Eight silicon modules went under the camera, picked to span decades of production. The oldest was a Solarex panel built in 1978, with a module efficiency of 5.7 percent. At the other end, a Trina module from 2022 reached 21.1 percent.

A modern cell turns far more of its current into light, and by that measure a better cell is simply a brighter one.

Cells in the old panel averaged 560 mV of open-circuit voltage and a luminescent quantum efficiency of 0.0007 percent. Cells in the newer module ran at 687 mV and 0.23 percent.

Across those 45 years the quantum efficiency climbed by roughly a factor of 300.

The model had to fit both ends of that span of solar power manufacturing, and it mostly did. Readings landed where the 60 mV rule predicts, apart from four modules built with multicrystalline solar cells, which trap light less well than single-crystal ones.

Those four only fell into line after the team accounted for the difference.

Werner’s team didn’t put the camera up against an industrial one. The claim is narrower. A model of how a camera turns photons into numbers lets an ordinary one produce absolute values.

The weak points are the ones the authors name themselves. A box drawn by hand over each cell picks up the dark gaps between them, which put scatter in the data. An automatic version still has to be written.

Every measurement was made in the dark, on silicon, on modules whose performance was already known. And the whole scale depends on that single reference cell.

So far the authors haven’t pointed the calibrated camera at a module nobody has measured. That test would show whether the method grades hardware or only confirms what was known.

“Our next step is to use the calibrated camera to determine quantum efficiencies and open-circuit voltages of further, previously uncharacterized solar cells and modules,” Werner said.

He added that the same model should work on a panel lit up rather than powered up, and possibly outdoors.

Daylight matters most. The method needs a dark room, which keeps it in a laboratory. Outdoors, the camera could be carried along a row of panels at a working solar farm.

The full study was published in The Journal of Applied Physics.

Kimber Digital Securities gains broker-dealer status for tokenized assets

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Plume affiliate plans to offer tokenized US stocks, ETFs and bonds through self-custodial wallets, with traditional shareholder rights preserved.

Kimber Digital Securities, an affiliate of blockchain network Plume, said Tuesday it had registered as a broker-dealer with the US Securities and Exchange Commission and joined the Financial Industry Regulatory Authority.

In its announcement, the company said it plans to offer tokenized US stocks, exchange-traded funds (ETFs) and bonds that investors could hold in self-custodial wallets. The company gave no launch date.

Tokenized stocks are digital tokens that represent company shares or rights tied to those shares. Transfers of the tokens are recorded on a blockchain. The rights investors receive depend on how the tokens are structured.

Unlike wrapped securities, the proposed tokens would represent existing ownership rights recorded through the Depository Trust Company (DTC). The company said investors would retain their dividend and voting rights and could transfer eligible holdings between traditional brokerage accounts and onchain wallets.

The company also plans to offer yield-bearing investment products to stablecoin users, including tokenized shares in funds managed by major US asset managers.

Kimber’s registration comes as other companies prepare to launch around-the-clock trading of tokenized US securities. On Monday, Formation Securities announced plans for a Solana-based trading venue offering tokenized shares of Galaxy Digital, Exodus Movement and Forward Industries, with a launch targeted for early 2027.

Major US exchange operators are pursuing similar initiatives ahead of 2027. The New York Stock Exchange is developing a 24/7 tokenized securities platform using Avalanche for settlement, while Nasdaq is targeting the second quarter of 2027 for its tokenized equity initiative with Kraken’s parent, Payward.

The value of tokenized stocks onchain has surged more than sixfold over the past year, rising to $3.87 billion from $643.3 million in November 2025.

Ring’s AI-Powered Drone Automatically Patrols Inside Your Home

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Ring’s AI-Powered Drone Automatically Patrols Inside Your Home

Home security can now fly. Amazon-owned Ring has announced the Ring Drone Cam, a $250 indoor flying security camera designed to automatically patrol a home’s interior.

The AI-powered Ring Drone Cam works alongside existing Ring products, including static cameras and sensors. When an alarm sounds or motion is detected, the drone automatically deploys to investigate.

Homeowners can also launch the drone on command or program custom flight routines on a scheduled basis. Due to its compact, lightweight design, flight times are limited to eight minutes per charge. Users can monitor a live 360-degree feed on their smartphones while the drone is airborne, and built-in LED spotlights allow it to operate effectively in total darkness.

Although designed to operate autonomously, initial setup requires some manual effort: users must carry the drone throughout their home to help it build an internal spatial map. Additionally, Ring notes that navigation works best in environments with distinct visual details; reflective surfaces, featureless walls, and frameless glass may pose operational challenges.

Detailed camera specifications remain sparse, but Ring confirms the drone features a 2K (1920×1920) square image sensor paired with a 148-degree field of view.

After completing a patrol, the drone automatically returns to its base station, fully recharging in roughly an hour. Ring emphasizes that all video and audio recording automatically stops whenever the drone is docked.

With a subscription, users can store, share, and review recorded footage—including motion events and Live View feeds—for up to 180 days. Users can also search through archival footage using plain-text queries.

Designed exclusively for indoor use, the premise behind the Ring Drone Cam is simple: a single autonomous drone can monitor the same space as multiple stationary security cameras.

Ring founder Jamie Siminoff told The Verge during a launch event that the company has spent a decade developing the drone off and on, only recently achieving its design and performance goals after returning to the drawing board a year ago.

Although Amazon’s listing notes the device has not yet received FCC authorization, Siminoff states that the U.S.-assembled drone complies with all regulatory standards.

The Ring Drone Cam is scheduled to ship in January 2027 for $249.99.

How to get the Sinking Lure in Zelda Ocarina of Time

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Sinking Lure is an optional item you can find in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake, but the conditions for finding it are a little confusing. The lure itself isn't necessary for beating the game, and you can't even leave the fishing pond with it, so don't fret if this is something you're stuck on.

Below, we explain where to find the Sinking Lure in the Ocarina of Time remake, as well as what you can use the lure on.

Being honest, the conditions for getting the Sinking Lure to show up are a little confusing. We've explained our experiences across three different save files below.

For one, you may need to win the big prize in at least one time period. As young Link, you'll need to catch a that is at least 50 cm long to win the Piece of Heart, or as adult Link, you'll need to catch a fish that is at least 60 cm long to win the Golden Scale. We had a save file that had the Golden Scale but no Piece of Heart, and were able to find the Sinking Lure as young Link. Similarly, we have a save file where we have the Piece of Heart, but no Golden Scale, and we were able to find the lure as adult Link.

Additionally, the lure will actually only show up after you pay the Fishing-Pond Man the rupees and rent the rod. In our tests, he says something about the lure (either being against the rules or that it keeps showing up in his pond), whenever the lure does appear.

There are a few places it can spawn: by the smaller waterfall pouring into a stone fountain on the north side of the lake, at the larger waterfall on the south side of the lake, or by debris inside the pond.

We also noticed that when testing things out for this guide, if you leave the area and then immediately come back, the Sinking Lure will not show up again until you fully leave the map and come back. For example, we ran in and out of the shop a few times to try to find the various spawn locations, but the Sinking Lure never showed up again until we warped to the Temple of Time and hoofed it back to the fishing pond. When we returned, we found the Sinking Lure again right away.

Well, as the Fishing-Pond Man warns you, if you use the Sinking Lure on any of the regular bass in the pond, he will not give you a reward, with one exception. If you manage to actually catch the Hylian loach at the bottom of the pond, he'll give you 50 rupees.

The Sinking Lure makes catching fish much easier, except for the loach. However, the Sinking Lure does make it a bit easier to hook the loach, because they spend a majority of their time underwater. The Hylian loach will require a lot of patience to catch, as it jumps out of the water and changes directions a lot, and it just takes longer to reel in. That being said, catching the Hylian loach is mainly for clout, as you don't earn anything outside the purple rupee for nabbing it.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake is a little bit different than the original game, so if you need guides to help you through, we don’t blame you. We have walkthroughs for all the dungeons, from the Deku Tree to Ganondorf's Castle. We also have guides for the sillier, in-between parts of the game, like when you have to find Richard (the dog) or Cuccos.

For those going for 100% completion, we also have guides on where to find every Piece of Heart, every Gold Skulltula token, and where to plant every Magic Bean.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

2027 Bugatti Tourbillon

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

See all results for new 2027 Bugatti Tourbillon for sale near 29405

In the world of fine chronometry, a "tourbillon" is a mechanical device within a watch's interior that counteracts the pull of gravity to ensure utmost accuracy. And while the Bugatti Tourbillon's artfully penned curves and handcrafted interior are just as dazzling to take in as the movement of a luxury timepiece, this four-wheeled wonder is much more than a $4.6 million piece of automotive jewelry.

A bespoke, naturally aspirated, 8.3-liter V-16 engine nestled into its carbon-fiber body structure behind the driver cements that fact, churning out 986 horsepower on its own. The addition of three electric motors—developed by parent company and electric sports car maker Rimac—ratchets output up to 1775 horsepower, making the Tourbillon Bugatti's most powerful model yet. Inside, the Tourbillon eschews large infotainment screens and extensive driver aids for expertly milled switchgear, top-shelf materials, and a more connected driving experience—a choice that its Audemars-Piguet-wearing clientele will surely appreciate.

Expert Tip: When it comes to seven-digit hypercars that prioritize luxury craftsmanship and outright speed in equal measure, the Tourbillon is in a class of its own. However, if you want to drop a couple of commas on a car that can go like hell on a racetrack, Ferrari's newest F80 flagship will do the trick.

The Tourbillon was revealed just last year. As a result, we don’t expect any notable changes to Bugatti's flagship for 2027.

If this year's model is largely unchanged and you're comfortable with a lightly used car, you may want to consider shopping previous model years. A one-to-three-year-old model will likely have a warranty and it'll cost less than buying new.

The Tourbillon is technically a hybrid, but the star of the show is a naturally aspirated 8.3-liter V-16 engine that's been designed with help from Cosworth and makes 986 horsepower and 664 pound-feet of torque. The big block is then paired with three electric motors, which boost combined output to an incredible 1775 ponies. The engine is backed by an eight-speed dual-clutch automatic transmission and all-wheel drive. We haven't had the chance to drive one yet, but we're obviously eager to do so. When we get the chance, we'll update this story with driving impressions and test results.

Car and Driver calculates your car's value in three free and easy steps - using the same Black Book® data dealers use to appraise vehicles.

Speaking of test results, here's one we can't wait to confirm: Bugatti says the Tourbillon can reach 60 mph in as little as 2.0 seconds and hit 250 mph in about 25 seconds. With all of that power, it might even be able to do the 60-mph dash even quicker in our testing.

In addition to the Tourbillon's three electric motors, this hypercar also carries a roughly 21.0-kWh battery pack that holds enough charge for about 30 miles of electric range.

EPA fuel economy estimates still aren't out yet for the Tourbillon. Even though it does boast a plug-in hybrid powertrain, the Tourbillon is not likely to be a high-mpg achiever. The Chiron, for reference, earned EPA ratings of 9 mpg city, 14 mpg highway, and 11 mpg combined. The Tourbillon may do slightly, but at the end of the day, it's a 1775-hp exotic, not a Prius. For more information about the Tourbillon's fuel economy, visit the EPA's website.

First Row: The interior of the Tourbillon is sculptural and mechanically fascinating, with a fixed-hub steering wheel that rotates around the gauges, so it never obscures the instrument cluster. In a collaboration befitting the Tourbillon's inspiration, a Swiss watchmaker builds the gauges, which feature raised numbers and physical needles. There's no plastic anywhere to be seen; even the clear coverings over the gauge face and center console are made of machined sapphire glass.

Cargo Capacity: While Bugatti says that the Tourbillon has more cargo space than the Chiron, that isn't saying much. The Chiron's trunk provided just two cubic feet of cargo space.

The Tourbillon was designed to be its own entertainment, but for those desperate to check texts or have a GPS connection, Bugatti has a small hide-away screen and Apple CarPlay connectivity.

The Tourbillon does not offer additional driver-assistance features, such as blind-spot monitoring. For more information about the Tourbillon's crash-test results, visit the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration (NHTSA) and Insurance Institute for Highway Safety (IIHS) websites.

Bugatti offers a four-year warranty on all Tourbillon models and covers maintenance for the vehicle over the same time period.

New in Civitas: "The Blue Tsunami Threatens To Drown Originalism"

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Mostly law professors | Sometimes contrarian | Often libertarian | Always independent

"Going forward, the path of advancing the conservative legal movement requires a political reorientation."

My new essay in Civitas tries to make sense of how the midterm election affects the broader conservative legal movement. Here is the introduction:

President Donald Trump's surprise 2016 election saved Justice Antonin Scalia's seat and perhaps the Supreme Court. Over the ensuing decade, Trump appointed scores of originalist judges at all levels of the judiciary and led a political movement that supported originalist state court judges and attorneys general. But that movement came to a screeching halt with the 2026 midterm election. Indeed, the blue tsunami, largely a referendum on Trump, threatens to drown originalism. In deep red states, conservative judges were washed off the bench. Also swept from office were conservative governors who appoint judges and attorneys general who litigate in those courts. Given the large Democratic Senate majority and unfavorable maps for 2028 and 2030, a Republican Senate with the votes to confirm originalist judges may not return until 2033 at the earliest. An entire generation of talent will be wasted. A necessary condition for a successful legal movement is a successful political party. Legal conservatives need to carefully reconsider the political circumstances best suited to defend the Constitution.

The conservative legal movement has always existed in conjunction and disjunction with the broader conservative political movement.  Certain long-term projects concerning the separation of powers and individual rights endure regardless of who is in office, but legal disputes arise with the exigencies of the day. The conservative legal movement will often, but not always, coalesce around current conservative political needs. Likewise, the conservative political movement will often, but not always, coalesce around the conservative legal movement. During two recent events, Justice Elena Kagan recognized this dichotomy. She observed that conservative members of the Supreme Court decide cases that are right "out of the playbook of the conservative legal movement," even as some of President Trump's policies suffer setbacks in the courts. Indeed, Trump has repeatedly expressed frustration that his appointees rule against him.

Many legal conservatives came to terms with voting for Trump in 2016 as a way to save the Supreme Court. Many more legal conservatives came around to Trump in 2020 based on his track record of appointing originalist judges--although I think that record is overstated. When Trump was on the ballot in 2024, some legal conservatives supported Trump for fear that Kamala Harris would pack the Supreme Court. But 2026 altered the calculus. Trump, and those candidates he endorsed, were routed in red states. Moreover, conservative judges and attorney generals were wiped from office. When the top of the ticket is no longer able to win elections, downballot judges and attorneys general do not stand a chance. Red states can only bleed for so long until they turn blue. A necessary condition for a successful legal movement is a political party that can produce viable candidates. Going forward, the path of advancing the conservative legal movement requires a political reorientation. The conservative legal movement can only survive if conservative lawyers are in public office to move the law. The blue tsunami cannot be allowed to drown originalism.

NEXT: School Districts' Negligence and Nuisance Claims Against Social Media Companies Rejected by California Court of Appeal

Josh Blackman is a constitutional law professor at the South Texas College of Law Houston and the President of the Harlan Institute. Follow him @JoshMBlackman.

2027 Mercedes-Benz Sprinter

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

See all results for new 2027 Mercedes-Benz Sprinter for sale near 98032

Seeing the Mercedes-Benz badge affixed to the front of a large commercial van feels, well, wrong, but the Sprinter van is a global leader in cargo hauling. It’s anything but a luxury-oriented Mercedes, instead focusing on package delivery and worksite practicality. A 170-hp diesel inline-four is standard, as is rear-wheel drive. A more powerful 211-hp version of the diesel is available and can be had with all-wheel drive.

The Sprinter is offered in several configurations: the Cargo Van model has seating for two and a crazy amount of cargo capacity behind the front seats. The Passenger Van variant can be had with up to five rows of seats to maximize people space. The Crew Van splits the difference and comes with two rows of seats with plenty of room behind the second row for your team’s tools of the trade.

Expert Tip: If you’re interested in an electric cargo van, Mercedes-Benz also makes the eSprinter, but we review that model separately.

Compare the 2027 Mercedes-Benz Sprinter with the top vehicles in this segment with our new compare tool.

The entry-level Worker trim for the Sprinter Cargo Van has been discontinued. Otherwise, the Sprinter carries over to 2027 without any other significant changes.

If this year's model is largely unchanged and you're comfortable with a lightly used car, you may want to consider shopping previous model years. A one-to-three-year-old model will likely have a warranty and it'll cost less than buying new.

Every Sprinter, save for the electric eSprinter, relies on diesel fuel. The base engine is a turbocharged 2.0-liter four-cylinder rated for 170 horsepower and 295 pound-feet of torque. The optional high-output engine is good for 211 ponies and 332 pound-feet. Both feed a nine-speed automatic transmission. All-wheel drive is available only with the higher-output engine.

While not as potent as the 310-hp EcoBoost V-6 in the Ford Transit or the 401-hp V-8 in the GMC Savana, the Sprinter's diesels offer adequate thrust. Though no van boasts razor-sharp handling, the Sprinter's standard load-adaptive stability control and available crosswind-assist systems help keep it out of trouble in dicey situations.

Car and Driver calculates your car's value in three free and easy steps - using the same Black Book® data dealers use to appraise vehicles.

In Comparison: Though we've yet to strap our test equipment to a recent Sprinter, we can tell you this is not a quick vehicle. We'd guess the base model would need 13.5 seconds to reach 60 mph, while the higher-output model might take 12.0. For reference, the Ford Transit can complete the same sprint in as little as 6.7 seconds.

The Sprinter can tow up to 7500 pounds when equipped with the high-output engine. Models with the 170-hp four-cylinder are good for 5000 pounds, though wheelbase and other specs can affect that cap.

The Sprinter's maximum payload is 6812 pounds. In order to reach that figure, you'll have to spec the cargo van with the heavy-duty 4500 chassis, the standard roof, the 144-inch wheelbase, rear-wheel drive, and the high-output engine. Crew models can carry as much as 3825 pounds, while the passenger van's hauling capacity tops out at 3307 pounds.

The EPA doesn't require automakers to submit fuel-economy data for full-size vans, nor does it publish such information. When we take a Sprinter on our 200-mile real-world highway fuel economy route, we'll update this section with the results.

First Row: Like other full-size vans, the entry-level Sprinter is spartan inside, but the driver's seat is heated, cruise control comes standard, and Benz offers several extra-cost upgrades to improve comfort, convenience, and driver safety.

Second, Third, Fourth, and Fifth Rows: The Sprinter cargo van has no seats after the driver and front passenger, but the Crew model gets a three-seat second row. Standard-length (144-inch) passenger vans can comfortably accommodate 12 people across four rows, while the 170-incher passenger van seats 15 across five rows.

Cargo Capacity: The cargo van offers up to 533 cubic feet of space for whatever you need to haul. Crew vans can swallow a max of 396 cubes, while the passenger van (when fully occupied) tops out at 117 cubic feet.

The Sprinter is not your typical Mercedes. It's built for utility first and foremost, which helps explain the base model's underwhelming tech setup. Mercedes provides an AM/FM radio that plays through five speakers (13 in passenger vans).

Shoppers can option a 10.3-inch touchscreen, though, and that system supports wireless Apple CarPlay and Android Auto connections.

The majority of the Sprinter's driver-assistance features are optional, not standard.

For information about the Sprinter's crash-test results, visit the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration (NHTSA) and Insurance Institute for Highway Safety (IIHS) websites.

The Sprinter's limited warranty is typical for the class, but its powertrain coverage is longer than most. Mercedes doesn't include scheduled maintenance.

Grab This Trader Joe's Fast Food Dupe For A High-Protein Breakfast

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Trader Joe's is always showing you things you didn't know you needed until you set foot inside the store. A plethora of gummy candies? Check. Enough tasty frozen dinners to keep you from using your stove for a month? Absolutely. A variety of frozen items for less than $5? Without a doubt. Though they didn't make the cut on our official list of best frozen goodies, included in that last category are its English Muffin Breakfast Sandwiches, a high-protein fast food dupe that is a savory treat for your morning meal, or any time you need a quick, filling snack. Better still, you'll be able to tell your family that we have McDonald's Sausage McMuffins with Egg in the freezer.

Much like the aforementioned Sausage McMuffin with Egg, the TJ's version, which sells in a box of two for $4.99, has both a sausage and egg patty and a bit of melted cheddar cheese, placed between two slices of English muffin. And while it doesn't quite have the same amount of protein — 18 grams to the McDonald's sandwich's 20 — it nonetheless provides a very reasonable amount of the macronutrient, which is anywhere from 10 to over 30% of the average recommended daily intake.

In addition to keeping you feeling fuller longer as you go about your morning, these Trader Joe's breakfast sandwiches are also a joy to eat. One commenter on Reddit shared that they would definitely buy them again, writing that while they could make their own version, "these are quick, easy and [have] minimal cleanup[, so they're] perfect for a busy morning." We'd also add that you don't have to wait in a drive-thru line for them, either.

There are indeed heating instructions on the box of the Trader Joe's English Muffin Breakfast Sandwiches, for both the microwave and the air fryer. However, people on the internet have tried following the directions and ended up with results that were ... less than satisfactory. For one, we'd recommend skipping the microwave altogether; this method of heating food and bread products doesn't tend to work very well (bread usually emerges from the microwave tough and chewy). We know: It's the quickest method, and if it's all you have time for, go ahead and use it, but if you're not in a huge rush, the air fryer is much better (and you don't have to worry about anything dripping through the perforations, like you would with batter-y or runny foods).

That said, even the air fryer instructions might require a little tweaking, as one person on that same Reddit thread wrote that they tried cooking them in the super-hot appliance according to the directions, but that it needed a minute or so less than instructed. It's also important to note that if you want to heat up the contents of the box, which are two breakfast sandwiches, and you can't fit both sandwiches in your basket open-faced (which is the suggested way of cooking them), heat them one at a time for best results, rather than crowding the cooking surface.

Lastly, some report the sarnies need a little snazzing up. With that in mind, we suggest adding a dash of your favorite hot sauce, or maybe even a little ketchup or barbecue, just to add a little interest. It is a perfectly blank canvas, after all!

Heliene and Caelux combine PV cells and solar glass for a hybrid module

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Heliene, a solar modules manufacturer, and Caelux Corporation, a perovskite solar technology company, are starting commercial production of their Hybrid-Tandem solar module.

This module combines perovskite glass from Caelux and silicon N-type cells from Heliene, placing two energy generating sources in a single solar panel. Heliene is producing the modules in its plant in Mountain Iron, Minnesota, with first deliveries slated for 2027.

“One of the advantages of Caelux’s technology is that we can integrate its Energy Producing Glass into our existing manufacturing lines without having to build an entirely new production setup,” said Martin Pochtaruk, CEO and founder of Heliene. “With both companies manufacturing in the U.S., we’re bringing together two fully domestic supply chains to advance what’s possible with American-made solar, besides the fact that partnering with true friends also makes it fun.”

The companies are reporting a 27% efficiency from the Hybrid-Tandem module. Heliene and Caelux desiged this module to have a higher power density in a smaller project footprint.

“Heliene is a true leader in U.S. manufacturing, and we share a belief that when we lead the market, our customers win. Together, we’re commercializing ultra-high-efficiency solar modules that will set a new bar for American-made solar,” said Caelux CEO Scott Graybeal.

Heliene will showcase the Hybrid-Tandem module at RE+ in Las Vegas from Nov. 17 to 19, at booth #W16546.

Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

Near AI has disclosed a breach that exposed account information belonging to about 100 users and data from 18 AI agents after an attacker exploited software running its servers.

The company said in a November 9 report that an attacker used a remote code execution vulnerability in SGLang, an open-source engine that runs AI models and generates responses to user requests.

The exploit allowed the intruder to execute commands on one inference server and then reach other parts of Near AI’s infrastructure.

Investigators traced the intrusion to an internal system connected to Private Chat, a feature the company had already retired. The attacker downloaded names and email addresses for approximately 100 users, along with subscription plan and account balance information for some of them.

Another 18 agents were accessed on an older deployment of Near AI Agents, where the attacker retrieved their chat histories, settings and logs. Those agents were not running inside NEAR AI’s confidential computing environment, which is designed to shield information from the company operating the servers.

Logs also exposed NEAR AI Cloud API keys belonging to two customers. These keys function like digital credentials that applications use to access cloud services; Near AI says it has revoked both, but did not say whether the attacker used them.

Near AI found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files had been accessed. The confirmed theft involved account records and legacy agent data, while exposure of live confidential requests remains a possibility identified by the investigation.

Near AI is part of the broader Near ecosystem, which has been promoting AI privacy alongside its blockchain ambitions. Its Cloud product uses trusted execution environments, or TEEs, to keep AI prompts encrypted and isolated from server operators, even while models process them.

Hardware isolation does not guarantee that the software inside the protected environment is safe. “It does not protect against a vulnerability in the software running inside the environment,” Near AI acknowledged, explaining that the SGLang flaw let the attacker run code where confidential requests were being processed.

That is particularly awkward for a business selling privacy as a core feature. In October, Near AI announced that Brave’s Leo browser assistant was using its confidential inference infrastructure for a supported AI model, although the incident report does not identify Brave users among those affected.

SGLang had already attracted scrutiny from security researchers. In July, Carnegie Mellon University’s CERT Coordination Center documented six vulnerabilities in the project, including bugs that could let attackers execute commands or expose credentials, depending on how servers were configured.

Near AI did not identify the specific SGLang flaw exploited in this incident, so there is no basis to link the breach to any of those earlier disclosures. It reported the vulnerability to SGLang’s developers.

Near, to its credit, was quick to fix the issues.

“On detection, we revoked all active sessions and administrative credentials, replaced all agent tokens, took Private Chat offline, and deployed a fix for the SGLang vulnerability across all inference servers,” the project said.

”We've completed the rotation of all remaining credentials and a full forensic review. We have notified users whose account details or agents we have confirmed were accessed and will make any regulatory notifications that are required.”

Near AI notified users whose account records or agents it confirmed were accessed and says it will make any required regulatory notifications. It also warned users to be wary of unsolicited messages referring to their Near AI accounts or balances, and contacted the owners of all 18 affected agents individually.

AI firms sign Macron’s Paris Call, but safety group says it lacks teeth

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

OpenAI, Anthropic and Microsoft promise shutdown switches and outside testing, but there are no shared risk limits.

The Paris Call meeting at the Élysée Palace on 9 November, with leaders and AI executives joining by video

OpenAI, Anthropic, Microsoft and Mistral are among ten AI companies that have signed a new declaration on the safety of advanced AI models. The Paris Call for AI Safety and Security was agreed on Monday at a meeting hosted by French President Emmanuel Macron.

L’intelligence artificielle avance très vite. On l’a déjà vu : cela implique des risques réels pour notre sécurité collective.

Notre travail pour developper nos technologies doit aussi être celui de l’anticipation et de la maîtrise de ses risques.

J’ai réuni à Paris et en visio une cinquantaine d’acteurs : chefs d’État et de gouvernement, dirigeants des plus grands laboratoires d’IA, chercheurs et experts.

Le constat est partagé. Il faut agir ensemble. Nous avons obtenu des engagements de tous autour de trois priorités : mieux évaluer de façon indépendante et publique les risques des modèles les plus avancés, mieux partager les alertes en cas d’incident grave et responsabiliser les acteurs qui développent des modèles.

Notre ambition est claire : faire de l’IA un progrès pour tous, avec une sécurité garantie par l’engagement de chacun.

— Emmanuel Macron (@EmmanuelMacron) November 9, 2026

The announcement came on the opening day of the Paris Peace Forum, where AI was one of the main topics of discussion.

Six countries signed the declaration: France, Canada, Finland, Norway, the Netherlands and Bahrain. The other participating companies are Hugging Face, Cohere, Black Forest Labs, Domyn, Sakana AI and Synthesia. Yoshua Bengio’s AI safety organisation LawZero is among the research groups supporting it.

Around 50 people joined the discussions by video, including OpenAI chief executive Sam Altman, Anthropic chief executive Dario Amodei and Microsoft president Brad Smith.

US Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent also took part, although the US is not among the signatories. Google, Meta and China have not signed the declaration either.

Macron said AI was advancing rapidly and posed “real risks to our collective security”, calling for governments and companies to work together to address them.

The signatories have committed to developing fail-safe mechanisms that can shut down their AI models, including during training and internal use. They will also provide clearer information about what their systems can and cannot do, and allow qualified independent experts to test them where appropriate.

The declaration also calls for a shared approach to assessing and managing the risks of frontier AI. This would cover malicious uses, including cyberattacks and weapons development, as well as the possibility of AI systems acting independently or moving beyond human control. Non-sensitive test results would be published in a common format.

Governments and companies will also share early warnings about serious incidents, while whistleblowers will have access to secure channels for reporting concerns.

The commitments have drawn criticism from French AI safety organisation CeSIA, which signed the declaration but argues that it leaves important gaps.

It sets no common risk thresholds for deciding whether a model should be developed or released, and does not guarantee that independent testers or governments will have access to the systems they need to assess.

CeSIA also wants governments to have the authority to suspend a model when its risks become too high. It argues that developers need clearer obligations and a timetable for meeting them.

“These decisions must rest with public authorities,” said Charbel-Raphaël Ségerie, CeSIA’s executive director.

The Paris Call follows an initiative launched by Norway and Finland in September to bring frontier AI under greater oversight, which France later joined. It also builds on earlier global safety commitments made by AI companies in Seoul and discussions at the G7 summit hosted by France in Evian.

The declaration remains open to other signatories, but it sets no deadlines. The shared testing framework it proposes has yet to be developed.

On Tuesday, the forum turned to another concern for Macron: the effects of AI on children. Speaking at a summit on children in the AI era, the French president called for AI tools to undergo checks before being made available to young users.

“We will push for pre-certification of all artificial intelligence solutions,” he said.

Macron compared the idea to the safety checks required for children’s toys, arguing that parents would not give a child an untested toy and that AI tools should be held to the same standard.

He also warned that the effects of AI on young people’s mental health were already becoming apparent, as more children and teenagers turn to chatbots that can influence their emotions and how they see the world. A charter on safer AI for children was due to be signed later on Tuesday.

President Emmanuel Macron also received a report, titled “Protect without preventing”, from a government-appointed panel of experts examining generative AI and human vulnerability.

I write about startups, venture capital, digital policy, and Europe ecosystem, with an eye on the larger story beneath them: who gets to build the future, who profits from it, and how Europe is learning to speak in a louder voice of its own. Mauricio Pochettino Appointment Can Save Lisandro Martinez's Man Utd Career

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are in a crisis, and the board look set to make the decision that a change is necessary.

United sit 18th in the Premier League with just one win in 10 Premier League games so far this season, while they have won just 3 of 14 in all competitions under Carrick, and GIVEMESPORT sources understand that the iconic former midfielder is set to be relieved of his duties at Old Trafford as a result.

Speculation has been rife for several weeks about his future, and who could potentially replace him, and it has now emerged that USMNT boss Mauricio Pochettino is currently a leading candidate for the role.

The Argentine has got plenty of Premier League and Champions League experience, having managed Southampton, Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain, and Chelsea before taking up his role as boss of the United States, where he led the team to the Last 16 at the 2026 World Cup, before being rewarded with a new four-year deal.

But that contract is understood to have a $10m (£7.4m) release clause, which could open the door for him to return to England once again, and finally get the Man Utd job he has been pining for since 2019.

His arrival would be a mark of intent from INEOS, and potentially a final roll of the dice for board members like Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada, who have so far failed to live up to their reputation of being 'best in class' since being brought in.

However, on the pitch, his appointment could also be a saving grace for some players or a dagger in the heart for others, who may not fit in with his style of play or how he sees football.

One player who could potentially be handed a reprieve at Old Trafford under the 54-year-old is his fellow countryman, Lisandro Martinez, who has been strongly linked with an exit away from the club in 2027.

The central defender has been in terrible form in recent weeks, having been at fault for a number of goals, before a ridiculous red card against Chelsea led to the team's collapse in a 3-0 defeat at Stamford Bridge. But more than that was his public outburst against the club hierarchy, where he admitted to feeling let down by the summer business and the strength of the squad. He has been labelled as being part of a 'toxic' culture at the club by pundit Adam Joseph.

Martinez is out of contract at Man Utd at the end of the season, and while the club have the option to extend by a further 12 months, reports have suggested they will not be looking to trigger that.

But with Pochettino being a manager who likes to dominate possession and build up from the back, the quality that Martinez has and his leadership qualities will likely be huge for the coach when it comes to implementing his style in the short-term.

Martinez had been a firm fan favourite until this season, and should he be able to string together a run of top performances and United start to turn the season around, he would likely be back in the good books quickly. That could force INEOS' hand when it comes to his contract situation, and could also convince the player that he is at the right club after all.

With funds understood to be limited, finding a replacement immediately for Martinez will be difficult. Pochettino won't want to be handicapped anymore if he is stepping in mid-season, and that could be the perfect remedy for a reprieve.

I’ve waited a long time for the Boox Picco, and I’m not disappointed

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

At around $100, the Boox Picco offers one of the most portable e-reader experiences you can get. You add MagSafe-like capability with a refreshingly simple UI, and you have a delightful way to read, literally wherever you are.

There is an argument to be made for every single shape and size e-readers come in, and they come in so many different sizes it’s hard to keep track. I love the idea of having a 10-inch display that I can skim, but even if it’s perfect for sitting at home and reading anything from novels to comics, I don’t think it provides a very good travel experience

But even smaller devices like the Boox Palma make you choose between bringing a second device or sticking with your phone. Most often, the phone wins out – especially when you have a flexible approach with a foldable like the Galaxy Z Fold 8 I’ve been using daily.

Unlike the other phone-sized e-readers from Boox, the Picco stands at just 4.15 inches tall. The screen measures at just under that – 3.97 inches. It’s essentially the size of a MagSafe or Qi2 wallet – just small enough to fit on the back of your phone with the included magnetic case.

It’s thin, too. A 5.5mm side profile means it slips anywhere a minimal wallet would without issue. That goes for pockets just as well as crossbody bags; perfect for travel.

And that’s where I think I find so much more joy in using this over what would otherwise be considered a small e-reader, like the Palma. I don’t have a lot of excuses not to carry it around when I’m on the go. I’ll throw it in my Bellroy bag when I’m carrying what I’d considered my minimal EDC. It slides into any internal pocket, and it weighs next to nothing.

The back of the Boox Picco isn’t magnetic, but the included case is. If you want the thinnest possible footprint, forego the case. But adding it lets you do one of the coolest – and slightly copium-inducing – things with your phone: attach the e-ink device directly to your OLED-laden phone.

It does not work well with an unconventional device like my Galaxy Z Fold 8, but if you have nearly any other device like the Pixel 11, you should get a rather perfect fit. The Picco squares up to the back of any slab phone with Qi2 magnets, whether internal or via a case.

But, why? Why slap another device and add weight/thickness to your phone when it’s perfectly capable of displaying e-books?

Because it’s cool! That’s the driving reason. You can make the argument that it doubles your phone’s usability or that it gives you an easy place to store the tiny e-reader. The truth is that it does add extra thickness to your phone, and using your phone with the Picco piggybacking along can be uncomfortable, since your fingers now rest on another display and not a case or phone backing.

You can get used to it, and, as unnecessary as it may seem, it’s a fun way to carry around a much more eye-friendly screen.

The Picco diverts from other Boox devices in another way. It doesn’t run Android, which means you have no access to third-party apps like Google Play Books, Amazon Kindle, or any other store/library. Instead, the Picco uses a companion app to transfer EPUBs from your phone to the tiny e-reader.

I initially opposed this idea when I got a sneak peek during IFA in Berlin this year. But after using it for a few weeks, I’ll admit I’ve come around. But that all comes down to how you consume your media.

I try to source EPUBs no matter what device I use. On Android-powered e-readers, that means downloading Google Play Books and reading from there; I have my EPUBs uploaded to my own Google Play Books library, so they’re available universally.

On the Picco, my approach hasn’t changed much. I take those same EPUBs I have in storage and transfer them from the companion app.

I can understand how the lack of third-party libraries would make the Boox Picco moot. Still, I’ve been a proponent of buying digital books I can store in my own drives, so I can read them wherever, whether that’s the Google Play Books library or from a device like the Picco.

All of that is a roundabout way of saying the Picco, though it doesn’t let you use apps to read, makes it easy to get digital copies loaded and ready to flip through.

It uses a handshake connection that requires you to open the companion app on your phone, hit connect, and do the same on the Picco. Once connected, transferring EPUB files is as easy as selecting them from your phone’s physical or cloud storage and queueing them up. The process takes seconds, and the only issue I’ve had was solved by a force stop and trying again.

The homescreen is simply a carousel of your library, with a delightful little progress bar under each title as they’re sorted. You can access and manage the bookshelf from that page, as well as the minimal “apps” and settings. I appreciate the focus app and reading stats, but the countdown and to-do options require using the companion app. I don’t see the point of those.

It doesn’t even have a control panel. Swiping down from the top surfaces screen brightness and color controls. Even though this little guy is just over $100, it still has a front light controlled by a physical switch on the side. Compare that to e-readers at much higher price points without that game-changing feature.

The buttons opposite don’t control volume. Instead, they’re there for page turning.

It’s just simple and well-designed. Features are there to make reading a better experience, down to granular text controls for your digital books. It does exactly what it needs to do – nothing more, nothing less.

As far as battery goes, it meets my expectations. It’s not massive, so the battery is small in comparison to a smartphone. It still holds its own easily, with an easy week of use if you’re reading for an hour or two a day. Beyond that, it takes little time to charge and does great on idle, which is perfect for such a passive device.

The Picco will hit home for you if you want an ultra-portable option for your e-books. The same goes for those who don’t mind the smaller screen and lack of Android or third-party app support.

I get genuine joy every time I pick it up to read where I left off, and I think that’s because it’s so simple and nothing about it feels bloated, even the companion app, which is also straight to the point.

I think it’s been a while since a device has offered that experience for me. Adding the form factor, optional magnetic attachment for your phone, and price tag just gets me to that conclusion sooner.

The Boox Picco is the most adorable e-reader I’ve used, and it’s $109 on Amazon.

Epic responds after Gears of War creator claims it won’t give back the first character he created

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Epic Games refused to hand Gears of War creator Cliff Bleszinski the rights to Jazz Jackrabbit, though he claimed neither side had the paperwork.

The hare hasn’t exactly been gathering dust, either. More than three decades after Bleszinski first doodled him, Epic slotted Jazz into Fortnite Chapter 7 Season 4 as the Jackrabbit Sprite, which grants players an extra mid-air jump. So when his creator came asking for him back, Epic dug in.

In a November 7 post on X, Bleszinski wrote: “After lawyering up Epic claims they fully own the rights to Jazz Jackrabbit and won’t back down in spite of not having any paperwork to prove it (nor do I).”

Still, he pointed out that Epic pays him “from sales of it on GoG (which means paperwork exists somewhere)” and is “still happy to monetize him in FN,” referring to Fortnite.

Epic, however, stood by its position. In a statement to Dexerto, the company said: “Epic owns the Jazz Jackrabbit IP. We pay 100% of revenue generated by the Jazz Jackrabbit games on Gog.com to the four individuals who developed the game, including Cliff Bleszinski.”

The statement didn’t address the missing paperwork or his claim that he created the character before joining the company.

To make his case, Bleszinski also dredged up his first high school sketches of the gun-toting rabbit, drawn when he was around 17. When Limited Run Games CEO Josh Fairhurst asked whether the doodles proved the character predated Epic, Bleszinski replied that one would think so.

He joined Epic in 1992, and the rollicking platformer, coded by Arjan Brussee, launched in 1994 as the studio’s biggest seller at the time. Today, the game’s GOG listing names Epic as developer and publisher and identifies Jazz Jackrabbit as an Epic trademark.

Epic orders devs to use more AI after delaying Marvel x Star Wars extraction shooter: Report

Gears of War creator says Palworld “validates” his rejected pitch to Epic Games

Fortnite adds “Suggestive Themes” to ESRB rating after wave of thirst trap skins & jiggle physics

In a statement to IGN, Bleszinski said, “Jazz was my first hit and will always have a spot in my heart.” Even so, he said any future Jazz-like project would have to take the spiritual sequel route. “Business is business, I suppose,” he wrote.

A spiritual sequel would join a growing wishlist, after Bleszinski revealed earlier this year that he still wants to make a game that mixes John Wick and Carrie.
